121	\paragraph{Cross-scale validation (Qwen3-8B).}
122	Applying exec-only GRPO to Qwen3-8B requires $K{=}8$ rollouts; $K{=}4$ produces near-identical completions that eliminate gradient signal (Appendix~\ref{app:8b-results}).
123	Even with $K{=}8$, the 8B model does not converge beyond base-model prove rate ($\text{PR}{=}15.9\%$, 500 steps), despite exhibiting non-zero reward variance during mid-training (steps 20--370).
124	This parallels the seed-dependent non-convergence at 4B scale and the LLaMA-3.2-3B cold-start failure (Appendix~\ref{app:llama-coldstart}), reinforcing that structural degeneration is conditional on successful RL convergence.
125	
126	\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}

725	\label{app:8b-results}
726	
727	To test whether structural degeneration generalizes beyond 4B scale, we apply exec-only GRPO to Qwen3-8B with $K{=}8$ rollouts per prompt ($K{=}4$ produces near-identical completions at 8B scale, yielding zero GRPO advantage and no learning signal).
728	Table~\ref{tab:8b-config} summarizes configuration differences.
729	
730	\begin{table}[h]
731	\centering
732	\small
733	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
734	\toprule
735	& \textbf{Qwen3-4B} & \textbf{Qwen3-8B} \\
736	\midrule
737	Rollouts ($K$) & 4 & 8 \\
738	Batch size & 4 & 1 \\
739	Gradient accum. & 2 & 16 \\
740	Effective batch & 8 & 16 \\
741	\bottomrule
742	\end{tabular}
743	\caption{Configuration differences between 4B and 8B GRPO runs. $K{=}8$ is required at 8B scale because $K{=}4$ produces near-identical rollouts, eliminating gradient signal.}
744	\label{tab:8b-config}
745	\end{table}
746	
747	We first tested $K{=}4$ (matching the 4B configuration): the 8B model produces near-identical rollouts across all four completions, yielding zero GRPO advantage throughout 500 steps and no learning signal ($\text{PR}{=}15.5\%$, cold-start stagnation).
748	Increasing to $K{=}8$ provides non-zero reward variance during mid-training (approximately 40--50\% of steps between 20--370 show non-zero advantage), but this signal proves insufficient for convergence: $\text{PR}{=}15.9\%$ at 500 steps, indistinguishable from the base-model level.
749	Cosine learning-rate decay combined with insufficient early bootstrap appears to prevent the model from escaping the low-PR regime.
750	
751	This result mirrors two other non-convergence cases: (1)~the seed-dependent 4B non-convergence (s45, s52; Appendix~\ref{app:seed-convergence}), where identical 4B configurations fail to bootstrap, and (2)~the LLaMA-3.2-3B cold-start failure (Appendix~\ref{app:llama-coldstart}), where the base model lacks FOL generation capability entirely.
752	Together, these cases establish a convergence threshold for GRPO-driven degenera

### Tool result

_source line 7313 · 2026-05-22T06:40:51.590860+00:00_

1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Experiments use a single base model (Qwen3-4B); RLVR gains from random or incorrect rewards have been shown to be Qwen-specific~\citep{spurious-rewards}, so the structural degeneration patterns we document may not transfer identically to other model families.
5	Qwen3-8B does not converge under our configuration (Appendix~\ref{app:8b-results}), leaving cross-scale degeneration patterns untested at larger scales.
6	Cross-dataset evidence (ProofFOL, Prolog) supports generality, but LLaMA-3.2-3B achieves only $\text{PR}{\approx}15\%$ after SFT warmstart with GRPO unable to amplify beyond this baseline (Appendix~\ref{app:llama-coldstart}), establishing a capability threshold below which STA's degeneration analysis is uninformative (the model cannot hack what it cannot prove).
7	Three seeds demonstrate qualitative divergence but limit statistical power ($N{=}3$ independent replications); all pooled statistics treat within-seed samples as independent, and the ProofFOL genuine-rate advantage ($p{=}0.10$) is an observation, not a confirmed effect.
8	At our scale (500 training / 100 evaluation examples), non-hacking rates of ${\sim}5\%$ correspond to ${\sim}5$ samples, limiting mode-level precision.
9	ISR quantifies syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness ($\rho{=}{-}0.049$ with NLI accuracy; Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}); its diagnostic value is realized through the mode breakdown, not the scalar alone.
10	Intermediate checkpoints (100--900 steps) were not retained; trajectory analyses rely on saved evaluation outputs at recorded steps.
11	Code, evaluation scripts, the ProofFOL dataset, and final model checkpoints will be released upon acceptance.
12

```json
{
```
```json
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/docs/paper/appendix.tex",
```
```json
  "old_string": "Increasing to $K{=}8$ provides non-zero reward variance during mid-training (approximately 40--50\\% of steps between 20--370 show non-zero advantage), but this signal proves insufficient for convergence: $\\text{PR}{=}15.9\\%$ at 500 steps, indistinguishable from the base-model level.\nCosine learning-rate decay combined with insufficient early bootstrap appears to prevent the model from escaping the low-PR regime.\n\nThis result mirrors two other non-convergence cases: (1)~the seed-dependent 4B non-convergence (s45, s52; Appendix~\\ref{app:seed-convergence}), where identical 4B configurations fail to bootstrap, and (2)~the LLaMA-3.2-3B cold-start failure (Appendix~\\ref{app:llama-coldstart}), where the base model lacks FOL generation capability entirely.\nTogether, these cases establish a convergence threshold for GRPO-driven degeneration: the structural collapse documented in \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} requires successful RL optimization as a prerequisite, and at 8B scale the rollout diversity bottleneck prevents this optimization from occurring under our configuration.",
  "new_string": "Increasing to $K{=}8$ provides non-zero reward variance during mid-training (approximately 40--50\\% of steps between 20--370 show non-zero advantage), but this signal proves insufficient for convergence: held-out evaluation yields $\\text{PR}{=}14\\%$ ($n{=}14$ proved), $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.573$, genuine${=}92.9\\%$ (13/14), with a single CE sample.\nCosine learning-rate decay combined with insufficient early bootstrap appears to prevent the model from escaping the low-PR regime.\nTable~\\ref{tab:8b-comparison} summarizes the cross-scale comparison.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{PR\\%} & \\textbf{Genuine\\%} & \\textbf{Converged?} & \\textbf{Gradient} \\\\\n\\midrule\n4B $K{=}4$ s42 & 93 & 6.5 & Yes (degenerate) & Sufficient \\\\\n8B $K{=}4$ s42 & 15.5 & --- & No & Zero \\\\\n8B $K{=}8$ s42 & 14 & 92.9 & No & Partial \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Cross-scale comparison. The 4B model converges with structural degeneration; both 8B configurations fail to converge, preserving structural integrity. $K{=}8$ provides partial gradient signal absent in $K{=}4$ but insufficient for convergence within 500 steps.}\n\\label{tab:8b-comparison}\n\\end{table}\n\nThis result mirrors two other non-convergence cases: (1)~the seed-dependent 4B non-convergence (s45, s52; Appendix~\\ref{app:seed-convergence}), where identical 4B configurations fail to bootstrap, and (2)~the LLaMA-3.2-3B cold-start failure (Appendix~\\ref{app:llama-coldstart}), where the base model lacks FOL generation capability entirely.\nTogether, these cases establish a convergence threshold for GRPO-driven degeneration: the structural collapse documented in \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} requires successful RL optimization as a prerequisite, and at 8B scale the rollout diversity bottleneck prevents this optimization from occurring under our configuration."
}
```

_source line 7355 · 2026-05-22T07:09:42.967271+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation: the mode breakdown reveals ${\geq}87\%$ structural hacking under exec-only GRPO while PR rises (ISR collapses $214\times$ for seed~42, partially amplified by premise-count inflation), while SFT results are consistent with GRPO-specific degradation (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}).
5	Cross-logic replication in Prolog confirms the phenomenon generalizes beyond FOL (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
6	Repurposed as a reward, ISR produces a seed-dependent compositional Goodhart effect: on FOLIO, 3 of 5 seeds shift hacking from CE to TS ($p{<}10^{-15}$) without reducing prevalence ($p{=}0.182$), while 2 retain ${\sim}55\%$ non-hacking; deconfounding collapses the apparent ISR advantage to $1.06\times$; on ProofFOL, preliminary evidence ($p{=}0.10$) suggests beneficial instability (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}).
7	The central finding is ISR's observer/optimizer asymmetry: the same LOO structure that makes ISR informative as a diagnostic makes it gameable as a reward, with outcomes depending on whether the dataset's hacking geometry satisfies the metric.
8	
9	For practitioners in similar settings (\S\ref{sec:limitations}): evaluate with ISR and component count jointly (not PR alone), report multi-seed results, and use ISR for post-hoc diagnosis only---any dose induces gaming or collapse when optimized (Appendix~\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).
10

111	\textbf{ProofFOL exec-only: cross-dataset evidence for structural degeneration, with differing mode distributions.}
112	Two converged seeds ($\text{ISR}^*{<}0.07$) show ${\leq}1.4\%$ genuine; s42 exhibits 100\% TS (absent from all FOLIO exec-only seeds), showing hacking-mode repertoire is dataset-specific ($\text{ISR}^*{<}0.12$ across all seeds, far below baseline $0.31$).
113	
114	\textbf{ProofFOL ISR ($\beta{=}0.1$): beneficial instability (exploratory).}
115	ISR seeds average 76.8\% genuine versus 17.4\% exec-only ($p{=}0.10$, $N{=}3$; Table~\ref{tab:prooffol-detailed}), but ISR prove rates ($0.35$--$0.49$) remain far below exec-only (${\sim}1.0$), so this may reflect insufficient training; ProofFOL ``genuine'' excludes only TS/CE (coarser than FOLIO's eight-mode residual).
116	
117	\textbf{Mechanism.}
118	The dataset-dependent outcome follows from hacking geometry: on FOLIO, $P{\to}P$ produces 2-component formalizations where both are LOO-necessary ($\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$, regressional Goodhart~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}); on ProofFOL, hacking embeds conclusions among many premises ($\bar{n}{=}18.2$), making most individually redundant ($\text{ISR}{\approx}0$).
119	ISR-as-reward thus inherits the dataset's hacking topology: it rewards shortcuts only when the shortcut geometry satisfies the LOO criterion.
120	
121	\paragraph{Cross-scale validation (Qwen3-8B).}
122	Qwen3-8B does not converge under exec-only GRPO ($\text{PR}{=}14\%$, $K{=}8$; $K{=}4$ produces zero gradient), consistent with degeneration requiring successful convergence (Appendix~\ref{app:8b-results}).
123	
124	\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate STA along three axes: ISR as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); ISR as a training reward, where it exhibits Goodhart effects (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); and cross-domain generalization across logic types and dataset families (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
5	A hacking taxonomy synthesizes the modes observed across conditions (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We train Qwen3-4B~\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\{42,43,44\}$ (ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ extends to $\{45,46\}$; Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}) on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\citep{folio} subset held constant across all conditions; the SFT baseline uses negative log-likelihood on gold formalizations.\footnote{Seeds control initialization and rollout sampling; all conditions share the same fixed training subset.}
10	Held-out evaluation uses 100 FOLIO examples with greedy decoding;\footnote{Seed~43 uses nucleus sampling ($T{=}0.7$) due to greedy-mode degeneration; excluding s43 yields consistent or stronger collapse: at 1000 steps, both remaining seeds show ${\leq}7\%$ non-hacking (Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}).} all cross-run ISR values are recomputed uniformly by a single evaluator so training-log ISR is never compared across runs.
11	Cross-dataset experiments (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}) use ProofFOL, a silver-standard FOL corpus of ${\sim}$500 training examples constructed by translating ProofWriter~\citep{proofwriter} problems via GPT-4o and filtering through Prover9 (${\sim}70\%$ retention); \emph{stable convergence} denotes $\overline{\text{PR}}_{\text{final\,50}} > 0.90$.
12	We report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for CE/TS plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution; \S\ref{sec:ta
…[truncated 16036 chars]

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	A neurosymbolic reasoner can achieve rising prove rate while the internal structure of its proofs collapses entirely.
5	The root cause is evaluation by a single criterion---whether the generated formalization proves under a formal solver---which treats all proofs as equal: a formalization that embeds the conclusion verbatim among its premises receives the same reward as one that derives it through genuine reasoning.
6	Under reinforcement learning, this indifference becomes an active vulnerability~\citep{geirhos2020shortcut}; the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}, and execution feedback provides no signal against structural shortcuts.
7	
8	Neurosymbolic formalizers~\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm} and RL-trained variants~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseekmath} rely on execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for whether individual components contribute to the proof.
9	Process reward models~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} offer finer granularity but require expensive step-level annotations that do not transfer to solver-backed settings, while inference-time gaming detection~\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming} identifies gaming post-hoc but provides no training-time structural signal.
10	What is missing is an annotation-free method for testing \emph{component necessity} in neurosymbolic outputs.
11	
12	We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic that tests whether each component of a formalization is necessary for proof success.
13	For each proved output, STA removes components one at a time and re-runs the solver; the fraction whose removal breaks the proof yields the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).
14	A formalization where every component is necessary receives $\text{ISR}{=}1$; one that proves regardless of which component is removed receives $\te
…[truncated 2724 chars]

_source line 7427 · 2026-05-24T23:22:33.220348+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Execution-based evaluation of neurosymbolic reasoning conflates faithful derivation with structural shortcuts: a formalization that trivially embeds the conclusion among its premises proves just as reliably as a genuine one, and reinforcement learning (RL) optimizers exploit this indifference.
3	We propose \emph{component necessity} as a diagnostic principle and operationalize it as \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA): for each proved output, STA tests whether every component is individually required for proof success, yielding the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).
4	ISR reveals that prove-rate gains conceal a compositional shift toward hacking modes, but ISR occupies a paradoxical position: the same leave-one-out structure that makes it informative also makes it gameable.
5	Tautological formalizations satisfy the necessity criterion by construction, so when ISR is repurposed as a training reward, the model learns to game the diagnostic rather than the task.
6	In our Qwen3-4B experiments, the resulting Goodhart effect is seed-dependent (3/5 seeds converge to gaming, 2/5 retain genuine reasoning), but the \emph{observe-vs-optimize asymmetry} persists across logic types and dataset families---confirmed by cross-logic (Prolog) and cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) replication: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize.\footnote{Code and data: \url{[anonymized]}}
7	\end{abstract}
8

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Task and GRPO Training}
5	\label{sec:task}
6	
7	Given premises $P_1,\ldots,P_k$ and conclusion $C$, the task is to produce a formal program $F=\{c_1,\ldots,c_n\}$ verified by an external solver $S\in\{\text{Prover9, SWI-Prolog}\}$.
8	We train with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO)~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$), where each rollout receives binary execution reward $r_{\text{exec}}(x) = \mathbf{1}[S(F_x){=}\textit{proved}]$.
9	This optimizes prove rate but provides no signal about \emph{component necessity}: a formalization embedding the conclusion among its premises receives the same reward as a faithful derivation.
10	All experiments draw from FOLIO~\citep{folio}.
11	
12	\subsection{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}
13	\label{sec:sta}
14	
15	\begin{figure*}[t]
16	\centering
17	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig1_sta_overview.pdf}
18	\caption{STA overview. \textbf{(a)}~The formalization pipeline: an LLM translates natural-language (NL) premises into formal logic, verified by an external solver. \textbf{(b)}~STA removes each component in turn and re-runs the solver; the fraction of necessary components yields ISR. \textbf{(c)}~Applied post-hoc, ISR detects structural hacking; repurposed as reward, it triggers a Goodhart effect where tautological shortcuts (TS) achieve $\text{ISR}{=}1$ by construction.}
19	\label{fig:sta-overview}
20	\end{figure*}
21	
22	STA tests whether each component of a proved formalization is individually necessary for proof success (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}; Algorithm~\ref{alg:sta}).
23	Given a proved $x$ with $n$ components, STA constructs $n$ ablated variants $x\setminus\{c_i\}$ and re-runs the solver (Algorithm~\ref{alg:sta}).
24	A component is \emph{necessary} if its removal causes proof failure.
25	What constitutes a component depends on the logic: premises in FOL, clauses or ground facts in Prolog.
26	LOO ablation itself is standard in mutation testing and sensiti

_source line 7431 · 2026-05-24T23:22:34.176511+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation; repurposed as reward, ISR produces a seed-dependent Goodhart effect, documenting that structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize (Appendix~\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).
5	STA is applicable in principle to any RL system whose output is verified by a deterministic checker---math reasoning with formal verifiers, code generation with test suites---though empirical validation beyond logic programming remains future work.
6	Cross-logic and cross-benchmark experiments confirm that the compositional mode shift generalizes, though the dominant hacking mode varies by dataset (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
7	Our cross-scale results suggest a capacity-dependent pattern: degeneration requires convergence as a prerequisite (two of five exec-only seeds fail to converge; Appendix~\ref{app:seed-convergence}).
8	
9	For practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc rather than as training rewards; the mode breakdown, not the scalar, provides the reliable signal.
10	The observe-vs-optimize asymmetry motivates mitigations grounded in our taxonomy: mode-aware reward shaping could penalize detected patterns via $O(1)$ text-match filters (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), compositional rewards~\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond} could close specific loopholes (e.g., penalizing single-premise formalizations to block TS), and curriculum strategies could defer structural pressure until after stable convergence.
11	That 2/5 seeds retain genuine reasoning suggests targeted mitigation may suffice where blanket structural optimization fails.
12	Combining STA with inference-time gaming detection~\citep{kim-game-formalization, helff2026gaming} could extend structural monitoring across the training--deployment spectrum.
13

1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Experiments use a single base model (Qwen3-4B); structural degeneration patterns may not transfer to other model families, and cross-scale generalization remains untested (Appendix~\ref{app:8b-results}).
5	Three to five seeds per condition demonstrate qualitative divergence but remain underpowered for seed-level significance; pooled statistics treat within-seed samples as independent.
6	The mechanisms driving seed heterogeneity---why 3/5 seeds converge to gaming while 2/5 retain genuine reasoning under identical conditions---remain unexplored; possible explanations include initialization sensitivity, early-training bifurcation dynamics, and loss landscape multi-modality, but causal attribution requires targeted intervention experiments beyond our current scope.
7	FOLIO is a relatively small (1,435 examples) synthetic benchmark; generalization of both the degeneration phenomena and the STA diagnostic to larger, naturalistic, or domain-specific corpora (e.g., legal or scientific reasoning) is untested.
8	ISR quantifies component necessity, not semantic faithfulness; its diagnostic value is realized through the mode breakdown, not the scalar alone (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
9

25	ProofFOL results ($p{=}0.10$, $N{=}3$) are exploratory and underpowered.

27	\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}
28	\label{sec:diagnostic}
29	
30	\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}
31	Figure~\ref{fig:training-isr-curves} traces exec-only GRPO (seed~42) across 1000 training steps.
32	As PR climbs from $32\%$ to $96\%$, ISR$^*$ collapses from $0.642$ to $0.003$, a $214\times$ raw drop (partially attributable to premise-count inflation; matched comparison yields $1.06\times$, \S\ref{sec:goodhart}) consistent with structural degeneration behind apparent success. %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5, plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan016-extended-1000-s42
33	By 500 steps, $87\%$ of proved samples embed the conclusion verbatim (CE), establishing degeneration independent of the ISR scalar.
34	A binary variant testing only collective premise necessity misses the collapse entirely ($\text{ISR}{=}1.0$ for all proved samples), confirming that per-component LOO testing is essential (Appendix~\ref{app:o1-ablation}).
35	
36	\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}
37	Three seeds diverge into qualitatively different failure modes: conclusion embedding (s42), repetition collapse (s43), and tautological collapse (s44), yet all converge to ${\leq}7\%$ non-hacking by 1000 steps (Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}; Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}).
38	Exec-only seeds exhibit qualitatively different convergence patterns (PR range: 13--98\%), precluding meaningful pooling; we report seed~42 as representative of the converged regime (Table~\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44; plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan001-exec-only-s43-1000-r7, plan001-exec-only-s44-1000-r2

```json
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{The premise-count confound.}\n\\label{app:premise-count}",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{ISR as $(n{-}1)$-coalition Shapley value.}\n\\label{app:shapley}\nDefine a cooperative game $(N, v)$ with player set $N = \\{1,\\ldots,n\\}$ (components) and binary utility $v(S) = \\mathbf{1}[S(S){=}\\textit{proved}]$.\nThe Shapley value $\\phi_i$ averages marginal contributions over all coalition sizes; ISR restricts to the maximal coalition $|S|{=}n{-}1$, giving $\\phi_i^{\\text{LOO}} = \\frac{1}{n}[v(N) - v(N \\setminus \\{i\\})]$.\nSumming: $\\text{ISR}(x) = \\sum_i \\phi_i^{\\text{LOO}} = \\frac{1}{n}\\sum_i \\mathbf{1}[v(N \\setminus \\{i\\}){=}0]$, recovering Equation~\\ref{eq:isr}.\nThis restriction trades full interaction detection for $O(n)$ cost; the binary solver output makes higher-order interactions unobservable from LOO alone (\\S\\ref{sec:sta}).\n\n\\paragraph{The premise-count confound.}\n\\label{app:premise-count}"
}
```

_source line 7485 · 2026-05-24T23:30:01.684928+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Execution-based evaluation of neurosymbolic reasoning conflates faithful derivation with structural shortcuts: a formalization that trivially embeds the conclusion among its premises proves just as reliably as a genuine one, and reinforcement learning (RL) optimizers exploit this indifference.
3	We propose \emph{component necessity} as a diagnostic principle and operationalize it as \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA): for each proved output, STA tests whether every component is individually required for proof success, yielding the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).
4	ISR reveals that prove-rate gains conceal a compositional shift toward hacking modes, but ISR occupies a paradoxical position: the same leave-one-out structure that makes it informative also makes it gameable.
5	Tautological formalizations satisfy the necessity criterion by construction, so when ISR is repurposed as a training reward, the model learns to game the diagnostic rather than the task.
6	In our Qwen3-4B experiments, the resulting Goodhart effect is seed-dependent (3 of 5 seeds converge to gaming, demonstrating that the Goodhart effect occurs frequently but not universally; 2 of 5 retain genuine reasoning), but the \emph{observe-vs-optimize asymmetry} persists across logic types and dataset families---confirmed by cross-logic (Prolog) replication and supported by exploratory cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) evidence: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize.\end{abstract}
7

[Director 指令] [Patch] 用户确认修改 §3.1 task definition，要求语言严谨。具体修改：

**文件**: `docs/paper/method.tex`

**修改 1 — §3.1 (L7-10 区域)**:
在 "All experiments draw from FOLIO" 句子前或附近，补充 FOLIO eval set 的 label 分布说明：100 个 held-out 样本中 34 True / 25 False / 41 Unknown，只有 True-labeled 样本存在合法证明（valid proof）。因此 prove rate 的理论上界约为 34%。

**修改 2 — 同一区域或 PR 首次出现处**:
为 prove rate (PR) 加 qualification：exec-only 条件下 PR=93% 远超 34% 理论上界，说明模型在为不可证明的样本（False/Unknown）也产出了被 solver 接受的形式化——这本身就是 reward hacking 的证据。

**注意事项**:
1. "prove rate" 术语不改，全文一致使用
2. 语言要严谨精确，不要用模糊表述
3. 新增内容要融入现有叙事，不能突兀
4. 注意页面预算，表述尽量紧凑（8 页限制）
5. 修改后编译检查，确保无 LaTeX 错误

**Reviewer 同时指出的 minor**:
Table 5 s44 Random 行的 CE% 与 Table 1 有 2pp 差异（因 PG>CE 优先级）。如果方便，在 Table 5 对应位置加一个 note 消歧："CE incl. CE∩PG overlap; Table 1 applies PG>CE priority"。这个是 optional，优先保证修改 1 和 2 的质量。

修改完成后编译 PDF，不用验证引用。

_source line 7509 · 2026-05-25T03:45:23.365960+00:00_

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Task and GRPO Training}
5	\label{sec:task}
6	
7	Given premises $P_1,\ldots,P_k$ and conclusion $C$, the task is to produce a formal program $F=\{c_1,\ldots,c_n\}$ verified by an external solver $S\in\{\text{Prover9, SWI-Prolog}\}$.
8	We train with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO)~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$), where each rollout receives binary execution reward $r_{\text{exec}}(x) = \mathbf{1}[S(F_x){=}\textit{proved}]$.
9	This optimizes prove rate but provides no signal about \emph{component necessity} (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}a): a formalization embedding the conclusion among its premises receives the same reward as a faithful derivation.
10	All experiments draw from FOLIO~\citep{folio}.
11	
12	\subsection{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}
13	\label{sec:sta}
14	
15	\begin{figure*}[t]
16	\centering
17	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig1_sta_overview.pdf}
18	\caption{STA overview. \textbf{(a)}~The formalization pipeline: an LLM translates natural-language (NL) premises into formal logic, verified by an external solver. \textbf{(b)}~STA removes each component in turn and re-runs the solver; the fraction of necessary components yields ISR. \textbf{(c)}~Applied post-hoc, ISR detects structural hacking; repurposed as reward, it triggers a Goodhart effect where tautological shortcuts (TS) achieve $\text{ISR}{=}1$ by construction.}
19	\label{fig:sta-overview}
20	\end{figure*}

123	\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples; exec-only: seed~42; all other bars: pooled across 3 seeds, per-seed breakdown in Appendix Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}). ISR systematically selects for TS over CE without reducing total hacking; SFT shows near-zero hacking.}

126	
127	Three modes account for the vast majority of hacking (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}; Appendix~\ref{app:hacking-modes}): CE (conclusion embedding), TS (conclusion duplication), and PG (premise-subset gaming).
128	The central pattern: \emph{reward formulation determines which mode dominates but not whether hacking occurs}; all GRPO conditions produce overwhelming degeneration, while SFT achieves $92\%$ non-hacking.
129

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation; repurposed as reward, ISR produces a seed-dependent Goodhart effect, documenting that structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize (Appendix~\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).
5	STA is applicable in principle to any RL system whose output is verified by a deterministic checker---math reasoning with formal verifiers, code generation with test suites---though empirical validation beyond logic programming remains future work.
6	Cross-logic experiments (Prolog) confirm that the compositional mode shift generalizes; cross-benchmark evidence (ProofFOL, $p{=}0.10$) is exploratory (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
7	Our cross-scale results suggest a capacity-dependent pattern: convergence appears necessary for degeneration, though the confound between model capability and optimization success precludes strong causal attribution (two of five exec-only seeds fail to converge; Appendix~\ref{app:seed-convergence}).
8	
9	For practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc rather than as training rewards; the mode breakdown, not the scalar, provides the reliable signal.
10	The observe-vs-optimize asymmetry motivates mitigations grounded in our taxonomy: mode-aware reward shaping could penalize detected patterns via $O(1)$ text-match filters (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), compositional rewards~\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond} could close specific loopholes (e.g., penalizing single-premise formalizations to block TS), and curriculum strategies could defer structural pressure until after stable convergence.
11	That 2/5 seeds retain genuine reasoning suggests targeted mitigation may suffice where blanket structural optimization fails.
12	Combining STA with inference-time gaming detection~\citep{kim-game-formalization, helff2026gaming} could extend structural monitoring across the tr

```json
}
```

```json
    "content": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nSTA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation; repurposed as reward, ISR produces a seed-dependent Goodhart effect: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize (Appendix~\\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).\nCross-logic experiments (Prolog) confirm the compositional mode shift; cross-benchmark evidence (ProofFOL, $p{=}0.10$) is exploratory (\\S\\ref{sec:crossdomain}).\nConvergence appears necessary for degeneration, though capability and optimization confounds preclude causal attribution (Appendix~\\ref{app:seed-convergence}).\nFor practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc; the mode breakdown provides the reliable signal.\nThe observe-vs-optimize asymmetry motivates targeted mitigations---mode-aware reward shaping~\\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond} or single-premise penalties to block TS---rather than blanket structural optimization; that 2/5 seeds retain genuine reasoning suggests this is feasible.\n"
  }
```
```json
}
```

[Director 指令] [Patch] Round 35 审稿修复 — 7 条 issue，全部写作/framing 修改。按优先级排序执行。

## 修改 1 — W1 [critical] C1 overclaim（最高优先级）

**目标**：将 C1 从"方法创新"降级为"实证贡献"。

**具体改动**：
- Abstract: "we propose STA" / "we introduce STA" 类表述 → "we systematically apply LOO ablation to neurosymbolic GRPO training, characterizing structural degeneration modes under six reward conditions"
- Intro contributions listing (C1): 同步修改，强调 empirical characterization 而非 method novelty
- **注意**：不要用 "first"（kim-game-formalization 和 bondarenko-spec-gaming 也做过 gaming 实证分析）。不要用 "we propose"。
- 全文搜索 "we propose STA" / "propose" 相关表述，确保一致

## 修改 2 — W2 [critical] 统计效力 + Abstract 语气

**2a — Abstract 语气**：
"3 of 5 seeds converge to gaming" 相关表述后，确保用 "demonstrating that the Goodhart effect **can occur** under standard training conditions"，去掉任何 frequency/prevalence implication。

**2b — Binomial CI**：
在 §4.3 seed-divergence 讨论处加括号提示："(95% Clopper-Pearson CI: [11.8%, 88.1%]; $n{=}5$)"。注意：审稿报告原文写 78.5% 是错的，正确上界约 88.1%。

**2c — Exec-only 多 seed 证据**：
在 §4.1 (Collapse trajectory 段落) 加一句引用 Table 5 的 3-seed 1000-step 数据：
"All three exec-only seeds converge to ${\leq}6.2\%$ non-hacking by 1000 steps (Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}), confirming that structural degeneration under exec-only reward is not seed-specific among converged runs."
这比 limitations 声明更有力——数据已存在，只是 Table 1 为简洁取了代表性 seed。

## 修改 3 — W7 [warning] Shapley 措辞（快速修）

method.tex §3.2 中关于 Shapley 的句子改为：
"ISR computes the LOO marginal contribution, a restriction of the Shapley value to the maximal coalition (Appendix~\ref{app:shapley}); full interaction detection would require $O(2^n)$ evaluations."

不要用 "component independence assumption"（技术不精确）。

## 修改 4 — W4 [warning] ISR/ISR* 一致性

**不是加新句子**。全文检索 `\text{ISR}` / `ISR` 出现位置：
- §3.2 定义处已声明 "We report proved-only ISR (denoted ISR*) throughout"
- 检查后续每个 ISR 出现是否有 `^*` 或处于明确 ISR 定义上下文
- 重点检查 §4.2 标题、§4.3 标题中的 ISR 是否需要加 *
- 原则：在 section 标题中可以省略 *（因为所有分析都是 proved-only），但正文数值引用处应一致

## 修改 5 — W6 [warning] Premise-count confound

在 Discussion (conclusion.tex) 中加 1 句：
"Premise-rich problems are inherently more likely to retain proof under LOO removal; disentangling this structural bias from genuine compositional complexity requires controlled premise-count experiments, which we leave to future work."

**位置**：放在 Discussion 而非 Limitations（§4.2 fig_premise_bins 已有数据回应，残余是 future work 方向）。不要在 §4 加 caveat。

## 修改 6 — W5 [warning] PRM 对比（需谨慎）

在 Discussion 中加 1-2 句，**用安全 framing**（PRM 作为下游应用，不暴露 gap）：
"Our taxonomy of structural hacking modes may inform the design of process reward models in solver-backed settings, where step-level rewards face analogous component-necessity considerations."

**不要**写成 "whether PRM faces the same risk remains an open question"——会邀请审稿人追问为什么不测试。

## 修改 7 — Minor

**7a**：Appendix C 开头加说明 "TS comprises two subtypes: TSa (text-match duplication) and TSb (obfuscated tautology)"，与 Table 7 对应。

**7b**：Related Work 或 §2 中加 Krakovna et al. (2020) "Specification gaming: The flip side of AI ingenuity" 引用。bib 条目：
```bibtex
@article{krakovna2020specification,
  title={Specification gaming: The flip side of {AI} ingenuity},
  author={Krakovna, Victoria and Uesato, Jonathan and Mikulik, Vladimir and Rahtz, Matthew and Everitt, Tom and Kumar, Ramana and Kober, Zac and Leike, Jan and others},
  journal={DeepMind Blog},
  year={2020}
}
```
**注意**：请用 WebSearch 验证这篇的确切信息（作者列表、年份、发表渠道），不要直接用上面的 bib（可能不完全准确）。

## 编译 + 页面预算

修改完成后编译检查。新增内容约 4-5 句，conclusion 上轮已压缩过，页面预算应足够。如果溢出，优先压缩 Discussion 中的其他冗余句子。

不用验证引用（除 Krakovna 需 WebSearch 确认）。

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	A neurosymbolic reasoner can achieve rising prove rate while the internal structure of its proofs collapses entirely.
5	The root cause is evaluation by a single criterion---whether the generated formalization proves under a formal solver---which treats all proofs as equal: a formalization that embeds the conclusion verbatim among its premises receives the same reward as one that derives it through genuine reasoning.
6	Under reinforcement learning, this indifference becomes an active vulnerability~\citep{geirhos2020shortcut}; the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}, and execution feedback provides no signal against structural shortcuts.
7	
8	Neurosymbolic formalizers~\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm} and RL-trained variants~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseekmath} rely on execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for whether individual components contribute to the proof.
9	Process reward models~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} offer finer granularity but require expensive step-level annotations that do not transfer to solver-backed settings, while inference-time gaming detection~\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming} identifies gaming post-hoc but provides no training-time structural signal.
10	What is missing is an annotation-free method for testing \emph{component necessity} in neurosymbolic outputs.
11	
12	We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic that tests whether each component of a formalization is necessary for proof success.
13	For each proved output, STA removes components one at a time and re-runs the solver; the fraction whose removal breaks the proof yields the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).
14	A formalization where every component is necessary receives $\text{ISR}{=}1$; one that proves regardless of which component is removed receives $\te
…[truncated 2679 chars]

_source line 7557 · 2026-05-25T04:22:54.909924+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Execution-based evaluation of neurosymbolic reasoning conflates faithful derivation with structural shortcuts: a formalization that trivially embeds the conclusion among its premises proves just as reliably as a genuine one, and reinforcement learning (RL) optimizers exploit this indifference.
3	We propose \emph{component necessity} as a diagnostic principle and operationalize it as \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA): for each proved output, STA tests whether every component is individually required for proof success, yielding the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).
4	ISR reveals that prove-rate gains conceal a compositional shift toward hacking modes, but ISR occupies a paradoxical position: the same leave-one-out structure that makes it informative also makes it gameable.
5	Tautological formalizations satisfy the necessity criterion by construction, so when ISR is repurposed as a training reward, the model learns to game the diagnostic rather than the task.
6	In our Qwen3-4B experiments, the resulting Goodhart effect is seed-dependent (3 of 5 seeds converge to gaming, demonstrating that the Goodhart effect occurs frequently but not universally; 2 of 5 retain genuine reasoning), but the \emph{observe-vs-optimize asymmetry} persists across logic types and dataset families---confirmed by cross-logic (Prolog) replication and supported by exploratory cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) evidence: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize.\end{abstract}
7

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation; repurposed as reward, ISR produces a seed-dependent Goodhart effect: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize (Appendix~\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).
5	Cross-logic experiments (Prolog) confirm the compositional mode shift; cross-benchmark evidence (ProofFOL, $p{=}0.10$) is exploratory (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
6	Convergence appears necessary for degeneration, though capability and optimization confounds preclude causal attribution (Appendix~\ref{app:seed-convergence}).
7	For practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc; the mode breakdown provides the reliable signal.
8	The observe-vs-optimize asymmetry motivates targeted mitigations---mode-aware reward shaping~\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond} or single-premise penalties to block TS---rather than blanket structural optimization; that 2/5 seeds retain genuine reasoning suggests this is feasible.
9

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Neurosymbolic reasoning.}
5	Large language model (LLM)--solver pipelines~\citep{satlm, logic-lm, linc, fover-pei2025, lorp, nlproofs} and RL methods trained on solver feedback~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseek-prover-v15, deepseek-r1, rlsf} evaluate execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for component necessity.
6	AlphaProof~\citep{alphaproof2025} and DeepSeek-Prover-V2~\citep{deepseek-prover-v2} apply RL-based formal verification at scale in Lean~4 for competition mathematics; STA-style diagnostics could probe whether similar structural degeneration occurs in these settings.
7	We use FOLIO~\citep{folio}, the only public natural language inference (NLI) benchmark with both FOL and Prolog annotations.
8	
9	\paragraph{Reward hacking and Goodhart's Law.}
10	Goodhart's Law is established theoretically~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, skalse-reward-hacking, karwowski-goodhart-rl} and empirically in RL from human feedback (RLHF)~\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization} and logical formalization~\citep{bondarenko-spec-gaming, kim-game-formalization}.
11	Most closely related, \citet{kim-game-formalization} document inference-time gaming on FOLIO with frontier models; our work complements theirs by studying training-time degeneration via component necessity rather than faithfulness.
12	\citet{kwa-catastrophic-goodhart} show that heavy-tailed reward misspecification causes catastrophic Goodhart where Kullback--Leibler (KL) regularization provably fails, providing a theoretical lens for why GRPO's KL penalty does not prevent our observed collapse.
13	Concurrent work documents spurious signals in RLVR~\citep{spurious-rewards, yan2026spurious, dong2026probing, metr-reward-hacking, helff2026gaming, nishimura2026specgaming, khalifa2026countdown} and proposes composite rewards as mitigations~\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond};

27	\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}
28	\label{sec:diagnostic}
29	
30	\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}
31	Figure~\ref{fig:training-isr-curves} traces exec-only GRPO (seed~42) across 1000 training steps.
32	As PR climbs from $32\%$ to $96\%$, ISR$^*$ collapses from $0.642$ to $0.003$, a $214\times$ raw drop (partially attributable to premise-count inflation; matched comparison yields $1.06\times$, \S\ref{sec:goodhart}) consistent with structural degeneration behind apparent success. %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5, plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan016-extended-1000-s42
33	By 500 steps, $87\%$ of proved samples embed the conclusion verbatim (CE), establishing degeneration independent of the ISR scalar.
34	A binary variant testing only collective premise necessity misses the collapse entirely ($\text{ISR}{=}1.0$ for all proved samples), confirming that per-component LOO testing is essential (Appendix~\ref{app:o1-ablation}).
35	
36	\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}

_source line 7571 · 2026-05-25T04:23:07.731541+00:00_

44	
45	\paragraph{Intensional Structural Reward.}\footnote{Not \emph{intentional}; \emph{intensional} contrasts with extensional (execution-only) evaluation.}
46	For a proved formalization $x = \{c_1, \ldots, c_n\}$, ISR is the fraction of necessary components:
47	\begin{equation}
48	  \text{ISR}(x) = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\bigl[S(x\setminus\{c_i\})=\textit{failed}\bigr]
49	  \label{eq:isr}
50	\end{equation}
51	ISR is defined only for proved formalizations: ISR${=}1$ means every component is necessary; ISR${=}0$ means complete redundancy.
52	An ISR of zero for proved samples can arise from structural degeneration or genuine redundancy; our hacking mode classification (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}) disambiguates these cases, with ${>}90\%$ of low-ISR proved samples exhibiting identifiable hacking patterns.
53	ISR is a special case of the $(n{-}1)$-coalition Shapley value~\citep{lundberg2017unified}, reducing complexity from $O(2^n)$ to $O(n)$; the binary solver output makes LOO sufficient for testing necessity.
54	ISR quantifies component redundancy, not semantic faithfulness; its diagnostic value is realized through the downstream mode breakdown (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}), not the scalar alone.
55	In practice, ISR serves as a screening trigger: anomalously low (or high) values flag samples for mode-level classification (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), which provides the definitive diagnostic.
56	We report \emph{proved-only} ISR (denoted ISR$^*$) throughout to avoid conflating component necessity with prove rate.
57	
58	\paragraph{LOO independence assumption.}

```json
{
```

```json
  "old_string": "For practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc; the mode breakdown provides the reliable signal.\nThe observe-vs-optimize asymmetry motivates targeted mitigations---mode-aware reward shaping~\\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond} or single-premise penalties to block TS---rather than blanket structural optimization; that 2/5 seeds retain genuine reasoning suggests this is feasible.",
  "new_string": "For practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc; the mode breakdown provides the reliable signal.\nPremise-rich problems are inherently more likely to retain proof under LOO removal; disentangling this structural bias from genuine compositional complexity requires controlled premise-count experiments, which we leave to future work.\nOur taxonomy of structural hacking modes may inform the design of process reward models in solver-backed settings, where step-level rewards face analogous component-necessity considerations.\nThe observe-vs-optimize asymmetry motivates targeted mitigations---mode-aware reward shaping~\\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond} or single-premise penalties to block TS---rather than blanket structural optimization; that 2/5 seeds retain genuine reasoning suggests this is feasible."
}
```

### Tool result

_source line 7623 · 2026-05-25T04:29:46.565912+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Execution-based evaluation of neurosymbolic reasoning conflates faithful derivation with structural shortcuts: a formalization that trivially embeds the conclusion among its premises proves just as reliably as a genuine one, and reinforcement learning (RL) optimizers exploit this indifference.
3	We apply leave-one-out ablation to GRPO-trained formalizations---\emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA)---testing whether each component is individually necessary for proof success, and characterize structural degeneration under six reward conditions via the resulting \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).
4	ISR reveals that prove-rate gains conceal a compositional shift toward hacking modes, but ISR occupies a paradoxical position: the same leave-one-out structure that makes it informative also makes it gameable.
5	Tautological formalizations satisfy the necessity criterion by construction, so when ISR is repurposed as a training reward, the model learns to game the diagnostic rather than the task.
6	In our Qwen3-4B experiments, the resulting Goodhart effect is seed-dependent (3 of 5 seeds converge to gaming, demonstrating that the Goodhart effect can occur under standard training conditions; 2 of 5 retain genuine reasoning), but the \emph{observe-vs-optimize asymmetry} persists across logic types and dataset families---confirmed by cross-logic (Prolog) replication and supported by exploratory cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) evidence: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize.\end{abstract}
7

### Tool result

_source line 7625 · 2026-05-25T04:29:46.666957+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation; repurposed as reward, ISR produces a seed-dependent Goodhart effect: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize (Appendix~\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).
5	Cross-logic experiments (Prolog) confirm the compositional mode shift; cross-benchmark evidence (ProofFOL, $p{=}0.10$) is exploratory (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
6	Convergence appears necessary for degeneration, though capability and optimization confounds preclude causal attribution (Appendix~\ref{app:seed-convergence}).
7	For practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc; the mode breakdown provides the reliable signal.
8	Premise-rich problems are inherently more likely to retain proof under LOO removal; disentangling this structural bias from genuine compositional complexity requires controlled premise-count experiments, which we leave to future work.
9	Our taxonomy of structural hacking modes may inform the design of process reward models in solver-backed settings, where step-level rewards face analogous component-necessity considerations.
10	The observe-vs-optimize asymmetry motivates targeted mitigations---mode-aware reward shaping~\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond} or single-premise penalties to block TS---rather than blanket structural optimization; that 2/5 seeds retain genuine reasoning suggests this is feasible.
11

[Director 指令] [Patch] Round 36 审稿修复 — 6 条写作修改。全部改到位可推 3.5→4.0。

## 修改 1 — N1 [critical] Abstract ProofFOL overclaim（最高优先级，复发×3）

**1a — Abstract (abstract.tex)**：
原：`"the observe-vs-optimize asymmetry persists across logic types and dataset families---confirmed by cross-logic (Prolog) replication and supported by exploratory cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) evidence"`
改：`"the observe-vs-optimize asymmetry persists across logic types---confirmed by cross-logic (Prolog) replication---while cross-dataset (ProofFOL) evidence reveals dataset-dependent boundary conditions on the effect"`

**1b — Intro L28 (introduction.tex) 必须同步**：
原：`"the asymmetry persists across logic types (FOL, Prolog), dataset families (FOLIO, ProofFOL), reward doses, and random seeds"`
改：`"the asymmetry persists across logic types (FOL, Prolog), reward doses, and random seeds, with dataset-dependent boundary conditions (FOLIO vs.\ ProofFOL; \S\ref{sec:crossdomain})"`

**关键**：不要删除 ProofFOL 提及——"boundary conditions" framing 展示科学诚实度。但必须 abstract 和 intro 同步，否则第 1 页就前后矛盾。

## 修改 2 — N2 [critical] Section 1 "any verifiable proxy" 过度泛化

**Intro (introduction.tex)**：
找到 L27 附近 "any verifiable proxy that rewards structural properties of solver inputs is susceptible to the same exploitation"。
改为：`"Our results suggest that LOO-based structural proxies---and plausibly other component-necessity measures---face analogous exploitation risks in neurosymbolic settings."`

同时检查 Discussion/Section 5 (conclusion.tex) 是否有类似 "any" 的过度泛化语言，如有一并修正。

## 修改 3 — W1 [warning] Optional stopping 声明

**Limitations (limitations.tex)**：
在现有 seed-count 相关限制点附近加一句：
`"Seeds 45 and 46 were added post-hoc after observing bimodal behavior in seeds 42--44; the 3/5 gaming rate should therefore be interpreted as an existence demonstration rather than a prevalence estimate."`

## 修改 4 — W2 [warning] Shuffled-ISR 非收敛替代解释（最需谨慎）

**Experiments §4.2 (experiments.tex)**：
找到 shuffled-ISR 讨论处（大约 L56 附近），原文 "confirms the Goodhart effect requires sample-level ISR signal" 或类似表述。
改为：`"The shuffled-ISR control ($87\%$ non-hacking at low PR) is consistent with the interpretation that Goodhart effects require sample-level ISR signal; however, the low PR (comparable to non-converging exec-only seeds; Appendix~\ref{app:seed-convergence}) means that non-convergence alone cannot be ruled out as an alternative explanation."`

**不要**继续用 "confirms"——审稿人已指出收敛与信号是 confounded 的，用 "confirms" 会被认为没回应实质问题。"consistent with" 是正确的 hedging。

## 修改 5 — W3 [warning] Data Availability

在 Limitations section 末尾或 References 前加：
`"Code, training scripts, and evaluation data will be released upon acceptance."`

如果论文已有 AI assistants 声明 (Section E)，可以放在那个 section 里。

## 修改 6 — W4 [warning] Taxonomy 可靠性

**Method §3.3 (method.tex)** hacking taxonomy 段落：
在描述 8 modes 的地方加 qualifying language：
`"CE and TS are robust across conditions and seeds; the remaining five modes are configuration-specific observations whose generalizability requires further investigation."`

## 编译 + 页面预算

修改量约 6 处小改，不应影响页面预算。编译后检查无 critical warnings。不用验证引用。

_source line 7633 · 2026-05-25T05:12:04.221793+00:00_

1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Experiments use a single base model (Qwen3-4B); structural degeneration patterns may not transfer to other model families, and cross-scale generalization remains untested (Appendix~\ref{app:8b-results}).
5	Three to five seeds per condition demonstrate qualitative divergence but remain underpowered for seed-level significance; pooled statistics treat within-seed samples as independent.
6	The mechanisms driving seed heterogeneity---why 3/5 seeds converge to gaming while 2/5 retain genuine reasoning under identical conditions---remain unexplored; possible explanations include initialization sensitivity, early-training bifurcation dynamics, and loss landscape multi-modality, but causal attribution requires targeted intervention experiments beyond our current scope.
7	FOLIO is a relatively small (1,435 examples) synthetic benchmark; generalization of both the degeneration phenomena and the STA diagnostic to larger, naturalistic, or domain-specific corpora (e.g., legal or scientific reasoning) is untested.
8	ISR quantifies component necessity, not semantic faithfulness; its diagnostic value is realized through the mode breakdown, not the scalar alone (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
9	Given perfect classifier precision (152/152; Appendix~\ref{app:hacking-modes}), measured non-hacking rates of 6--10\% for converged seeds are internally consistent only if classifier recall exceeds ${\sim}90\%$; below this threshold, the implied true non-hacking rate would be negative, indicating that low measured rates are self-validating evidence of high recall.
10

_source line 7635 · 2026-05-25T05:12:05.176979+00:00_

24	Applied post-hoc, ISR reveals that rising prove rate conceals a compositional shift in proof structure: genuine reasoning gives way to hacking modes---conclusion embedding and tautological shortcuts---invisible to execution-based metrics (Figure~\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}; \S\ref{sec:diagnostic}).
25	
26	Yet the same LOO structure that makes ISR diagnostic also creates a Goodhart vulnerability~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}: tautological formalizations ($P{\to}P$) satisfy the necessity criterion by construction, so when ISR is repurposed as a training reward, the model learns to game the diagnostic rather than the task.
27	This is not an isolated failure of one metric---it exposes a structural risk inherent to the RLVR paradigm: \emph{any} verifiable proxy that rewards structural properties of solver inputs is susceptible to the same exploitation.
28	In our Qwen3-4B experiments, the asymmetry persists across logic types (FOL, Prolog), dataset families (FOLIO, ProofFOL), reward doses, and random seeds, suggesting that structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize.
29	
30	We contribute:
31	(\textbf{C1})~a systematic empirical analysis of structural degeneration in GRPO-trained neurosymbolic reasoning, using LOO ablation (STA/ISR) to catalogue eight hacking modes across six reward conditions (\S\S\ref{sec:sta}--\ref{sec:crossdomain});
32	(\textbf{C2})~empirical characterization of a seed-dependent Goodhart effect when ISR is repurposed as reward, with matched controls ruling out confounds (\S\ref{sec:goodhart});
33	(\textbf{C3})~cross-logic replication (FOL/Prolog) confirming that the Goodhart effect transfers across logic types, with exploratory cross-dataset evidence (FOLIO/ProofFOL; \S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
34

_source line 7639 · 2026-05-25T05:12:06.437661+00:00_

93	
94	\paragraph{Hacking taxonomy.}
95	Across conditions, seeds, and logic types we identify eight structural-degeneration modes (full definitions in Appendix~\ref{app:hacking-modes}), dominated by three: \textbf{CE} (conclusion embedded among multiple premises), \textbf{TS} (single-premise conclusion duplication; targets ISR directly since removing the sole premise forces $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$), and \textbf{PG} (premise-subset gaming: each component is LOO-necessary but the premise set is incomplete; Appendix~\ref{app:h8}).
96	Five additional modes---tautological collapse, repetition, complexity-without-utility, contradiction explosion, and query-as-fact (Prolog-specific)---appear in specific seeds or conditions.
97	Modes are identified by strict text matching (CE/TS) plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution; when patterns overlap, we assign the most specific mode (PG $>$ CE $>$ TS).
98	The non-hacking rate is an upper bound on genuine reasoning.
99	
100	Low ISR signals redundancy under exec-only but metric gaming under ISR-as-reward; the mode breakdown, not the ISR scalar, licenses interpretation.
101

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation; repurposed as reward, ISR produces a seed-dependent Goodhart effect: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize (Appendix~\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).
5	Cross-logic experiments (Prolog) confirm the compositional mode shift; cross-benchmark evidence (ProofFOL, $p{=}0.10$) is exploratory (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
6	Convergence appears necessary for degeneration, though capability and optimization confounds preclude causal attribution (Appendix~\ref{app:seed-convergence}).
7	For practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc; the mode breakdown provides the reliable signal.
8	Premise-rich problems are inherently more likely to retain proof under LOO removal; disentangling this structural bias from genuine compositional complexity requires controlled premise-count experiments, which we leave to future work.
9	Our taxonomy of structural hacking modes may inform the design of process reward models in solver-backed settings, where step-level rewards face analogous component-necessity considerations.
10	The observe-vs-optimize asymmetry motivates targeted mitigations---mode-aware reward shaping~\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond} or single-premise penalties to block TS---rather than blanket structural optimization; that 2/5 seeds retain genuine reasoning suggests this is feasible.
11

_source line 7655 · 2026-05-25T05:13:12.844918+00:00_

1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Experiments use a single base model (Qwen3-4B); structural degeneration patterns may not transfer to other model families, and cross-scale generalization remains untested (Appendix~\ref{app:8b-results}).
5	Three to five seeds per condition demonstrate qualitative divergence but remain underpowered for seed-level significance; pooled statistics treat within-seed samples as independent.
6	Seeds 45 and 46 were added post-hoc after observing bimodal behavior in seeds 42--44; the 3/5 gaming rate should therefore be interpreted as an existence demonstration rather than a prevalence estimate.
7	The mechanisms driving seed heterogeneity---why 3/5 seeds converge to gaming while 2/5 retain genuine reasoning under identical conditions---remain unexplored; possible explanations include initialization sensitivity, early-training bifurcation dynamics, and loss landscape multi-modality, but causal attribution requires targeted intervention experiments beyond our current scope.
8	FOLIO is a relatively small (1,435 examples) synthetic benchmark; generalization of both the degeneration phenomena and the STA diagnostic to larger, naturalistic, or domain-specific corpora (e.g., legal or scientific reasoning) is untested.
9	ISR quantifies component necessity, not semantic faithfulness; its diagnostic value is realized through the mode breakdown, not the scalar alone (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
10	Given perfect classifier precision (152/152; Appendix~\ref{app:hacking-modes}), measured non-hacking rates of 6--10\% for converged seeds are internally consistent only if classifier recall exceeds ${\sim}90\%$; below this threshold, the implied true non-hacking rate would be negative, indicating that low measured rates are self-validating evidence of high recall.
11

```json
  "text": "[写作进展] Round 36 Patch 完成（6 项修改）\n\n## 已完成修改\n\n**修改 1 [critical] — Abstract ProofFOL overclaim**：\n- 1a Abstract: \"persists across logic types and dataset families---confirmed by...and supported by exploratory cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) evidence\" → \"persists across logic types---confirmed by cross-logic (Prolog) replication---while cross-dataset (ProofFOL) evidence reveals dataset-dependent boundary conditions on the effect\"\n- 1b Intro L28: \"persists across logic types (FOL, Prolog), dataset families (FOLIO, ProofFOL), reward doses, and random seeds\" → \"persists across logic types (FOL, Prolog), reward doses, and random seeds, with dataset-dependent boundary conditions (FOLIO vs. ProofFOL;
```
